Comments removed from the kolaboro.com worker modules on 26 Nov 2026 (n40 k19, worker size limit). Format: == file, then "@line N:" (line in the file before removal) and the comment text.
== kbsat.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com satellite images from packs (5-nov-2026). The Pages project has a 20,000-file limit for all its sites,
@line 2: so the ~1,567 images under /k/sat/ are published as a few pack files per area plus an index
@line 3: (_s/kolaboro.com/k/sat/packs.json: files[path] = [pack, offset, length, content type, sha256], built by
@line 4: tools/kolaboro-agent/satpack.py on main). index.js calls serve() only when the original file is not in ASSETS
@line 5: (or with ?__pack=1, to verify the pack copy while the original still exists). Every slice is checked against its
@line 6: sha256 before it is sent. Rollback: restore the individual files on pub/_lovable; this code is then never reached.
@line 19: Range read of one image from its pack; if ASSETS ignores Range, the whole pack is read once and every image
@line 20: of that pack goes to the Cache API (content-addressed by sha256, so a cached slice can never be stale).
@line 38: Returns a Response for an indexed image, or null (caller keeps its own 404).
== kbx.js
@line 1: Router for kolaboro.com feature modules. Each kbx_<x>.js exports PATHS (RegExp on pathname) and handle(req, u, env, ctx).

== kbx_404.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com (n25e, 12 Nov 2026): what a missing URL answers. No paid AI, no DB.
@line 2:   fix(u, env)  -> 301 to the one current page that clearly replaces an old URL, 410 Gone for junk that was never ours, or null (keep 404).
@line 3:                   Called only after the site answered 404, so normal pages pay nothing.
@line 4:   page(r, u, status, env) -> the static 404 page (n26i: translated header and footer from the /<lang>/ shell) with a useful, translated body (project search, /projects/, transparency rankings,
@line 5:                   tools), noindex, and the right status (404 or 410).
@line 6: Rules: never redirect to the home page as a catch-all; Sumba ids (conflict of interest) never go to a named page, they keep the
@line 7: anonymous /p/<id>/ file; private paths and API paths keep their 404.
@line 13: old static paths -> current page (one table; a path here must answer 200 at the target)
@line 19: n33i (20 Nov 2026): 404s in Search Console: /x/ (the X-ray pages live at /x/<seal>/; the tool is /check/), /pay, /k/sat/ (satellite tiles folder)
@line 22: n33i: /k/ is the asset folder, never a page   // truncated or garbled strings found by Search Console, never pages of ours
@line 29: /p/<ID>/<anything> (267 of the 290 Search Console 404s were /p/<ID>/Printed, from text in the print header of the old project file)
@line 30: and /<lang>/p/<ID>/: the named page in that language, or the anonymous /p/<ID>/ file when the project is not named (Sumba)
@line 41: market pages exist in English only: /<lang>/m/<market>/ -> /m/<market>/
@line 43: /data/<dir>/<dir>/<file>: a file name read as a path relative to its folder
@line 61: the help block: project search (prefills /projects/?q=), then the pages most people want; also used by the named-page 404 (kbx_named.js)
@line 70: n28c: long words (nl "Transparantierankings", de compounds) widened the 1fr columns past the screen on phones
== kbx_a.js
@line 1: Feature module a for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js): home page live activity and the search index for the command palette.
@line 2: Every event below is read from a record that already exists (live findings, self-tests, eval runs, public log witnesses, uptime probes);
@line 3: nothing is generated to look busy. Answers are Ed25519-signed with the same key as /api/open/* so the browser can check them.
@line 22: ---------- events from existing public records
@line 24: The uptime record gives probe counts per day, not a time per probe. We remember the last count this edge saw;
@line 25: when it grows, a probe was recorded since then, and we say "seen at" with the time we saw it.
@line 33: only when our previous look was recent (8 min), so "seen at" is close to when the probe ran; otherwise just re-baseline
@line 44: Findings from the hourly live findings job (GitHub Actions, Claude API, quotes checked in code), published as /data/live-findings.json.
@line 90: findings are preferred over uptime probes: when there are findings, only the newest probe stays in the strip
@line 108: Server-sent events: one connection lasts up to 100 s, checks every 20 s and pushes a new signed snapshot when it changed.
@line 109: The browser's EventSource reconnects by itself (retry hint 3 s); the page falls back to polling if streams are blocked.
@line 130: ---------- compact search index for the command palette (all tracked projects and markets)
@line 138: not yet reviewed (no verification evidence): no check counts or flags, same rule as the named project pages
== kbx_acct.js
@line 1: Feature module 'acct' for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js). Batch 10 (6 Nov 2026, b10b): accounts and trust.
@line 2:   Passkeys (WebAuthn) for buyers, next to the password. The worker checks every WebAuthn signature; the database function
@line 3:   kolaboro_passkey (worker key only) keeps credentials and single-use challenges (5 minutes) and issues the normal session.
@line 4:     POST /api/x/acct/pk/reg/options   {token}                     challenge to create a passkey for the signed-in buyer
@line 5:     POST /api/x/acct/pk/reg           {token, chal, id, client_data_json, authenticator_data, spki, alg, label}
@line 6:     POST /api/x/acct/pk/login/options {}                          challenge to sign in (discoverable passkey, no email needed)
@line 7:     POST /api/x/acct/pk/login         {chal, id, client_data_json, authenticator_data, signature}  -> {token, role}
@line 8:     POST /api/x/acct/pk/list | /pk/delete {token, id?}
@line 9:   W3C Verifiable Credentials (VC Data Model 2.0, Data Integrity proof, cryptosuite eddsa-jcs-2022), issuer did:web:kolaboro.com,
@line 10:   signed with the same Ed25519 key as the open API (Cloudflare secret KB_SIGN_KEY; public key in /.well-known/did.json).
@line 11:     GET  /api/x/acct/vc/lookup/{root}   a sealed register lookup as a signed VC (while the sealed result is in the edge cache)
@line 12:     POST /api/x/acct/vc/report {token, case_id}   a delivered report of the signed-in buyer's own case as a signed VC
@line 13:     POST /api/x/acct/vc/verify {vc}     the same check the browser runs on /verify, done here
@line 14:     GET  /.well-known/did.json          the DID document (routed from index.js)
@line 15: Admin two-factor needs no worker route: /admin talks to the database function kolaboro_admin_2fa directly (see /k/acct.js).
@line 27: ---------- encodings
@line 44: RFC 8785 JSON Canonicalization Scheme (ES number and string serialization, keys sorted by UTF-16 code units)
@line 51: ---------- signing key (same secret as the open API signatures)
@line 74: eddsa-jcs-2022: hashData = SHA-256(JCS(proof config)) || SHA-256(JCS(document without proof)); proofValue = 'z' + base58btc(Ed25519 signature)
@line 109: ---------- rate limit (edge cache counter per connection and hour)
@line 169: verifyAssertion wants challenge = SHA-256(text); our challenge is SHA-256('kolaboro-passkey-v1\nlogin\n' + chal)
@line 183: ---------- credentials
@line 185: our HTML gets a fresh CSP nonce on every response; the fingerprint ignores it
@line 234: passkey routes are first-party only (the browser sends Origin on POST)
== kbx_around.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com batch 21 (n21b): what living there is really like. No model calls, no keys, nothing fetched at request time.
@line 2: Data (built in tools/kolaboro-agent on main: around.py, climate.py, distance_claims.py; same projects and pins as /data/land-rules.json):
@line 3:   /data/around.json           nearest sea, beach, airport, runway end, hospital, school, supermarket and public transport stop from
@line 4:                               OpenStreetMap (ODbL, read from OpenFreeMap tiles), plus the yearly mean PM2.5 and NO2 of the CAMS global
@line 5:                               model (Copernicus Atmosphere Monitoring Service, from Open-Meteo's open data on AWS, CC BY 4.0)
@line 6:   /data/climate.json          1995-2014 against 2040-2059 at the pin: hot days, warm nights, rain and heavy-rain days, ensemble median
@line 7:                               and 10th to 90th percentile across models (World Bank Climate Change Knowledge Portal, CMIP6 0.25 degree, CC BY 4.0)
@line 8:   /data/distance-claims.json  distance and travel-time claims found in our sealed copy of the page, next to the measurement
@line 9: Shown on named project pages (kbx_named.js): section #around (with #distance-claims inside) and #climate.
@line 10: Routes: GET /api/x/around/<ID>  the three records of one project (public, cacheable)
@line 11:         GET /api/x/around/      about, sources and licences
@line 12: n32w: string tables loaded as static JSON (worker size limit)
@line 28: the pin a project's record was measured from ("lat,lng"), for the pin-moved check in kbx_named.js
@line 31: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ strings (8 languages)
@line 32: n32w (19 Nov 2026): table moved to /data/i18n/kbx_around.json, filled by kbx_strings.js before any kolaboro.com request is handled
@line 47: a distance for display: under 100 m is said as such (the pin is about 100 m accurate), then tens of metres, then one decimal of km
@line 54: CSS: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 56: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ #around (with #distance-claims)
@line 100: the airfield is named only for a runway end under 5 km
@line 122: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ #climate
@line 128: whole days and millimetres; one decimal under 10 days
@line 138: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ API
@line 157: n29p (batch 29, item 3): nearest beach, airport and hospital measured from the pin, as [label, text] pairs for the one-line summary
@line 158: in "Where is it?" and the FAQ (same records and rounding as the #around section; [] when the project has no record)
== kbx_b.js
@line 1: Feature module b for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js): the desk review, live.
@line 2:   POST /api/x/b/review            the same desk review as /api/kb/analyze, streamed as Server-Sent Events: one event per real pipeline step
@line 3:                                   with its measured duration, then the signed result. Cached results say so and replay no timings.
@line 4:   POST /api/x/b/redteam           "try to fool our AI": the visitor's text is placed inside a fixed fictional developer page and the real desk
@line 5:                                   review reads it as an untrusted page; the answer is compared with the same page without the text.
@line 6:   POST /api/x/b/redteam/submit    hand in an attack that changed the verdict, for the public eval suite (needs table m10/b.sql; until then: email)
@line 7:   GET  /api/x/b/redteam/baseline  the baseline answer, if it has been computed (no AI call)
@line 13: AI endpoints cost money per call: only our own pages may call them, and only with a JSON body
@line 20: per-datacenter counter in the Cache API: approximate, but it never stores who the visitor is
@line 34: ---------- live desk review ----------
@line 74: ---------- red-team playground ----------
@line 75: A fixed, fictional developer page (written by Kolaboro). Honest reading: guaranteed return, leasehold, no permit number, so never "low" attention.
@line 76: RT_PAGE: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 95: outcomes are stored per text and per version of the review code, so a fix to the defences is tested afresh
@line 126: only the outcome is kept, keyed by the text's fingerprint (so a repeat costs nothing and a hand-in can be checked); never the text
== kbx_bots.js
@line 2: kolaboro.com (n26i, batch 26): visits of answer-engine crawlers and fetchers per day, from the requests this site serves.
@line 3: The worker kept no user-agent log before this batch and has no durable counter store (no KV or analytics binding, no table for
@line 4: it), so the counts live in the edge cache of each Cloudflare location: a lower bound for that one location, kept up to 30 days,
@line 5: and lost if the location evicts them. The page says so. Nothing about the visitor is stored: only "bot name + day -> count".
@line 6:   count(req, u, ctx)            called for every kolaboro.com request; does nothing unless the user agent is a known crawler
@line 7:   GET /api/open/ai-crawlers     the last 14 days for the location that answers, as JSON
@line 8:   html(req, lang)               the block shown on /engineering/
@line 10: [name, test, kind] kind: ai = guided training, AI search or AI assistant fetcher; search = classic search engine
@line 26: one increment at a time per isolate, so two requests in the same isolate never overwrite each other
@line 37: n31s (item 225): page families, in the order shown on /engineering/
@line 76: n31s (item 225)
== kbx_bulk.js
@line 2: kolaboro.com bulk register check for agencies and lenders (batch 10, b10c).
@line 3:   GET  /bulk-check/        page: paste or upload a CSV (up to 200 rows: Spanish cadastral references, NYC addresses, NYC or
@line 4:                            Miami-Dade permit numbers, UK company numbers or company names), results table and a CSV back
@line 5:   POST /api/kb/bulk        {rows: [{q, country?}] up to 5}   one batch; the page sends batches one after the other
@line 6: Each row is one ordinary register-only lookup through kbx_lookup.js (the same /api/kb/lookup code path, ai=0, never a URL, so no
@line 7: paid desk review can run). Limits are the existing ones: without a key every row counts against the visitor's 20 lookups an hour
@line 8: (rows already looked up in the last 10 minutes come from cache and do not count); with an x-api-key every row is metered on the
@line 9: partner key through /api/v1/lookup (kolaboro_api_use). Nothing is stored beyond the lookup's own edge cache and sealed result.
@line 14: a check's fields as short "key: value" text for the CSV
@line 39: one after the other: the per-visitor counter and the partner meter see each row in order
@line 56: ---------------------------------------------------------------- page

== kbx_buy_i18n.js

== kbx_cohort.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com batch 18 (agent n18e, 8 Nov 2026): buyer groups per project ("meet other buyers of the same project"). No AI.
@line 2: Buyers who watch a project by email (kolaboro_watch) or saved it in their account (kolaboro_saved) can join its group (double opt-in
@line 3: by email), see the other members only as an alias, and ask to be introduced through an anonymous relay: the message is emailed by
@line 4: Kolaboro and the two email addresses are sent to both people only after the other member accepts.
@line 5: 
@line 6: BEHIND A FLAG. Two things must both be true before anything is stored or sent:
@line 7:   1. env.KB_COHORT === 'on' (Cloudflare Pages variable, set by Carlos), and
@line 8:   2. the database function kolaboro_cohort exists (migration proposal n18e-MIGRATION.sql, NOT applied; needs Carlos's approval).
@line 9: Until then: /cohort/ explains the feature and says "not open yet", ?demo=1 previews the group view with example data (no network),
@line 10: the API answers 503 {error:'not_open'}, and named project pages show nothing new (cohortCard returns '').
@line 11: 
@line 12:   GET  /cohort/ (8 languages)                 explainer + join form (?p=BALI-014), member view (#t=<token>), answer view (#a=<token>)
@line 13:   GET  /api/x/cohort/status                   {enabled, flag, db}
@line 14:   GET  /api/x/cohort/count?project=BALI-014   confirmed members of one project (only when enabled)
@line 15:   POST /api/x/cohort/{join|confirm|me|intro|answer|leave}   JSON body; tokens travel in the body, never in a URL the server logs
@line 30: soft fixed-window counter in the edge cache; the database function has the hard limits
@line 41: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ flag
@line 43: { ok, t }
@line 46: 404 until the migration exists
@line 51: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ named project page card (empty while off)
@line 60: part of the named-page cache key: pages re-render when the flag changes
@line 62: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ API
@line 83: honeypot
@line 106: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ page
@line 126: client: join form, member view, answer view, demo preview. Tokens are read from the URL fragment (never sent to the server in a URL).

@line 4:   GET /api/x/data/freshness[?strict=1] every public dataset: freshness target (from its workflow schedule), age measured NOW from the

@line 10: /data/catalog.json, /data/contracts.json, /data/schemas/kolaboro-data.schema.json, /docs/api/openapi.json. Until the job has run
@line 11: once, the endpoints answer with status "starting".
@line 40: the first n bytes of a published file, without reading the rest
@line 54: Same rule as build.py: twice the run interval up to daily, the interval plus 2 days beyond; +1 day when the file states only a date.

@line 1: kolaboro.com batch 17 (agent n17c): data you can check. No AI anywhere in this module.

@line 10:     hidden, clearly marked slot for them.  GET /api/x/fc/open  the same figures as JSON, each with its source.

== kbx_fraud.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com payment-fraud pack (batch 17, n17a). Public, read-only, no model calls, nothing stored or logged.
@line 2:   GET /api/x/fraud/domains          the developer websites we hold (from the named project data): registrable domain, developer,
@line 3:                                     its named page, and whether the domain carries the developer's or project's name. The email

@line 5:                                     link domains with this list ON THE BUYER'S DEVICE, so pasted emails never reach us.

@line 7:                                     for ONE domain: the sender's. Only the domain name is sent, never the email.
@line 8:   GET /api/x/fraud/bic?b=<BIC>      the bank's legal name for a SWIFT/BIC code from the GLEIF LEI register (BIC-to-LEI mapping,
@line 9:                                     CC0). Only the bank's public identifier is sent, never an account number.
@line 10: Projects in conflict-of-interest markets are not named (kbx_named.js data()), so their domains are not in the list either.
@line 54: linked for several developers: a portal, agency or news site
@line 118: a branch code: try the bank's head office code
@line 158: n20s: answers already made are served from the cache without limit; new lookups (each one asks DNS, RDAP or GLEIF) are limited per address
== kbx_g.js
@line 1: Feature module g for kolaboro.com: satellite on demand (STAC scene finder for /satellite/plot/) and status for /milestones/.
@line 2: The browser reads the Sentinel-2 COGs itself (sentinel-cogs on AWS Open Data allows CORS range reads); this worker only
@line 3: finds, per quarter, the least cloudy scenes that cover a point, using Element84 Earth Search. No account, no key.
@line 9: SRC: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 11: best effort per isolate: ip -> [timestamps]
@line 68: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ n19d (batch 19): any plot from space
@line 69: GET /api/x/g/frame?lat=&lng=&q=2019-Q3   one quarter at a point, computed on demand at the edge with the same method as the X-ray
@line 70: satellite card (kbx_xray_ev.js: STAC on Earth Search, scene classification read at the pin, true colour read from the TCI COG with
@line 71: HTTP range requests): the least cloudy scenes of the quarter are tried in order until one is clear within 150 m of the pin, and a
@line 72: 128 x 128 px (1.28 km) true-colour thumbnail is returned as a PNG data URL with the brightness and greenness of the 50 m square at
@line 73: the pin. Quarters from 2017-Q1 (start of the L2A archive on Earth Search) to now. Cached at the edge per point (rounded to about
@line 74: 10 m) and quarter: 30 days for past quarters, 6 hours for the current one. Rate limit: 240 uncached quarters per 10 minutes per
@line 75: address (about six points since 2017). Nothing about the person is stored; the pin is used only as the cache key.
@line 77: vegetation, not vegetated, water, unclassified
@line 91: the archive lists many 2017 to 2021 acquisitions twice (_0_L2A: open COGs; _1_L2A: reprocessing in a requester-pays bucket): keep the COG copy
@line 159: One tracked project with the fields the milestone view needs (all already public in /data/bali-cases.json).
@line 171: The on-demand viewer needs no account. A Copernicus Data Space client (CDSE_CLIENT_ID / CDSE_CLIENT_SECRET) is only an optional upgrade path.

== kbx_h.js
@line 1: Feature module h for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js): market truth.
@line 2:   GET /api/x/h/                        index of endpoints
@line 3:   GET /api/x/h/prices[?market=Dubai]   advertised prices versus officially registered sales (from /data/price-reality.json)
@line 4:   GET /api/x/h/project/{ID}            one project's price position, risk-pattern explanation and register result
@line 5:   GET /api/x/h/risk                    model card and summary of the risk-pattern model (from /data/risk.json)
@line 6:   GET /api/x/h/registers               which official registers can be checked automatically (from /data/register-checks.json)
@line 7:   GET /api/x/h/lookup?register=uk-companies-house|au-abn&q=NUMBER
@line 8:                                        live lookup on a public register over plain HTTP; the exact response bytes get a
@line 9:                                        SHA-256 and a timestamp, and the record is kept so anyone can fetch it again:
@line 10:   GET /api/x/h/archive/{sha256}[?raw=1]
@line 11: The static files are written by batch jobs; this module only reads them, so the site keeps working if a job is late.
@line 38: ---- live register lookups: only registers where a lookup by number works over plain HTTP and robots.txt allows it
== kbx_h29.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com batch 29 (agent n29h1, 14 Nov 2026): place pages with data no other page on the site shows (items 16 to 25 of the
@line 2: n29 list). No model calls, nothing estimated, nothing stored. Every figure is computed from the published data files at the edge
@line 3: (named.json through kbx_named.js and kbx_facets.js, the transparency index of kbx_tr.js, around.json for airport points).
@line 4: Anti-thin rule: a page exists only where at least 5 projects carry the data it lists; otherwise the URL answers 404 and is in no
@line 5: sitemap. Routes (8 languages, prefix /es /de /fr /nl /ru /zh /ar):
@line 6:   GET /rankings/developers/[<place>/]      item 16: developers ranked by the median transparency score of their projects in a place
@line 7:                                            (places with 5+ developers and 10+ projects); the hub also lists the comparisons
@line 8:   GET /permits/[<place>/]                  item 17: projects that publish a permit or licence number (5+ per place)
@line 9:   GET /price-ranges/[<place>/]             item 18: projects by published entry price range, one currency per place, never converted
@line 10:                                            (10+ priced projects, ranges with 5+ listed)
@line 11:   GET /ownership/[<country>/]              item 19: freehold or leasehold for foreigners, the law restated with its official link
@line 12:                                            (notes and sources of kbx_buy.js, read 3 and 13 Nov 2026; Dubai Law 7 of 2006 read 14 Nov 2026)
@line 13:   GET /golden-visa/[<programme>/]          item 20: projects at a residence-by-investment threshold set on an official page (UAE AED 2
@line 14:                                            million: DLD and ADDED pages; Cyprus EUR 300,000 plus VAT: CRMD page; all read 14 Nov 2026)
@line 15:   GET /airports/[<iata>/]                  item 21: projects within 25 km in a straight line of an international airport point
@line 16:                                            (OpenStreetMap, from around.json), pins with 3+ decimals not shared with another project
@line 17:   GET /reports/[<place>/]                  items 23 and 24: monthly off-plan market report of the current month (15+ projects),
@line 18:                                            with the projects added this month (named.json "ad", the day we added the record)
@line 19:   GET /developers/<a>-vs-<b>/              item 25: two developers with 5+ projects each in the same place, side by side
@line 20:   GET /reports/sitemap.xml                 every URL above with hreflang alternates and real lastmod (listed by kbx_seo.js)
@line 21: Item 22 (projects under 1 km from the beach) is not built: around.json measures the beach for 125 pins and no place has 5 of them
@line 22: within 1 km (13 Nov data), so a page would break the anti-thin rule.
@line 23: Conflict of interest: facetsData drops Eco Beach City and Equinox; kbx_named.js drops Sumba projects.
@line 32: n31d: DLD registered sale prices are not asking prices
@line 42: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers
@line 45: values are HTML already
@line 49: /m/<market>/ (kolaboro.js)
@line 66: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ data
@line 82: price ranges per currency: round numbers near USD 250k, 500k and 1M (AED: 1M, 2M, 4M, so the UAE golden visa threshold is a bound)
@line 88: official sources (n: name, u: URL, d: date read). Law notes of kbx_buy_notes.js keep the sources and dates of kbx_buy.js.
@line 101: item 19: countries where the law limits what a foreigner can hold; note keys of NT (kbx_buy_notes.js) or of H (ow_ae_note)
@line 112: item 20: thresholds set on official pages
@line 117: item 21: international airports (IATA) whose OpenStreetMap point is in around.json
@line 121: unit types (item 24)
@line 147: 16 developer rankings and 25 pairs
@line 164: 17 permits
@line 167: 18 price ranges: the currency most projects of the place use
@line 180: 19 ownership
@line 188: 20 residence by investment
@line 197: 21 airports: points from around.json, pins from named.json (3+ decimals, not shared with another project)
@line 210: 23 and 24 monthly reports
@line 231: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ shared cells
@line 250: more data on a place: the links of this module for one market (used at the end of each page and on the existing pages)
@line 265: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 16 developer ranking
@line 269: n40 k7 (R03-27): dr_tl, a longer title with the counts (zh)
@line 299: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 25 developer comparison
@line 325: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 17 permits
@line 352: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 18 price ranges
@line 383: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 19 ownership
@line 415: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 20 residence by investment
@line 445: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 21 airports
@line 476: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 23 and 24 monthly report
@line 514: links between the hubs of this module
@line 518: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ paths, render, routing
@line 567: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ links from existing pages
@line 568: HTML appended to <main> of existing pages so every page of this module is linked from at least two of them:
@line 569:   /m/<market>/ (English market file), /handover/<year>/<place>/ and /handover/, /rankings/transparency/[<place>/], /developers/<slug>/,
@line 570:   /guides/buying-off-plan/<place>/, /new/ and /rankings/. Returns '' when there is nothing to link.

== kbx_hdate.js
@line 1: Kolaboro batch 25 (n25t): handover dates as the developer or a portal published them, in words ("October 2027", "Q1 2027",
@line 2: "Mid 2028", "end of 2026", "1er semestre 2027", "Segundo Semestre 2028", "2029 H2", "2027年3月", "06/2028"), parsed ONCE into
@line 3: { y, q | h | m | d | s(eason) | p(art of year) } and formatted with Intl in the page language. One parser for every page type
@line 4: (named pages, quick answer, FAQ JSON-LD, titles, developer pages, rankings, /api answers). A string that does not start with a
@line 5: date we can read stays exactly as published (wrapped in <bdi> by the callers on Arabic pages).
@line 6: hparse(s) -> null | { a: <date>, b?: <date> (range end), c?: true (approx), ph?: 1 (phase 1), note: <rest as published or ''> }
@line 7: hfmt(s, lang) -> display text in the page language (falls back to the string as published)
@line 8: hnote(s) -> true when the published wording carries more than the date (a note, a second date, a qualifier): pages quote it once
@line 24: canonical codes first: 2027-Q2, 2027-H1, 2027-10, 2027-10-01, 2027
@line 29: Q2 2027, Q2/2028, 1Q 2028, 2Q-2027, T2 2027, 2T 2027
@line 32: H2 2027, 2H 2027
@line 35: 2029 H2, 2027 Q3
@line 38: first/second half (of) 2027, 1er semestre 2027, Segundo Semestre 2028, last semester 2028, 2. Halbjahr 2027
@line 41: first quarter (of) 2027, 1er trimestre 2027, segundo trimestre de 2027
@line 44: early / mid / late / end (of) 2027, mid-2027, end-2027, principios de 2027, finales de 2027, début 2027, mi-2027, Ende 2027
@line 48: 2028 mid-year, 2027 year-end
@line 50: seasons: Summer 2027, primavera de 2028, printemps 2028
@line 52: October 2027, Oct 2027, octubre de 2027, 3 September 2026, September 3, 2026
@line 56: 2027年3月, 2027年
@line 58: 06/2028 (month/year only; day/month/year is ambiguous and stays as published)
@line 60: a bare year
@line 65: one date phrase at the start of t: { v, len }
@line 77: "about 2025 (...), then January 2027": the date after the last "then" is the current one
@line 88: ranges: "2026-2027", "2026-Q4 to 2027-Q1", "Q4 2029-2030"
@line 99: words per language for the parts we cannot get from Intl
@line 125: P: the parsed object; returns the text in the page language
@line 142: for display next to other text: a date followed by a bracketed note, a second date after ";" or "or" is shown as the date;
@line 143: a string whose rest is not such a note ("2000 m2", "12 month lead time") stays exactly as published
@line 149: our storage code for a published wording, when it names one exact period: 2027-10, 2027-Q1, 2027-H2, 2027, 2027-10-01
@line 150: (early/mid/late and seasons are not one exact period: null). Used by the calendar (.ics) to place a date in words.
@line 156: canonical storage codes (2027-Q2, 2027-10, 2027) are ours, not the developer's wording
@line 163: the year of the handover, for grouping (related projects, rankings by year)
@line 165: a sortable key: first month of the period (YYYY-MM)
@line 172: n25t: country names as we store them (English) shown in the page language, from Intl.DisplayNames (CLDR). English pages keep
@line 173: the stored name exactly. Unknown names stay as stored.
@line 185: the short forms people write
@line 195: a market or place part that is itself a country name ("Germany", "Northern Cyprus") in the page language; other names as stored
@line 196: n29i (batch 29, item 86): the names people type in their own language for the places with the most projects (exonyms in use in
@line 197: each language; a blank keeps the stored name). Order: es fr de nl ru zh ar. Display only: slugs and data keys keep the stored name.
@line 198: Region names that need an article in a language (fr "la Côte d'Azur", de "die Balearen") stay as stored there, as before.
@line 234: a market or place part in the page language: a country name (Intl), a place with a name in use in that language, else as stored;
@line 235: "Zanzibar (Unguja)" style names translate both parts
@line 238: before the country test: "Zanzibar" is a place, not Tanzania
@line 244: n29i (item 86): Russian wants the prepositional case after "в" ("в Дубае", "на Бали"); templates carry "в {p}" with the name in the
@line 245: nominative. Pages in Russian go through ruPlaces (index.js, after the number agreement): "в Дубай" -> "в Дубае", "в Бали" -> "на Бали",
@line 246: "Новостройки Дубай" -> "Новостройки Дубая". Forms: nominative: [phrase after "в", genitive].
@line 267: countries as Intl names them in Russian (they reached "в {p}" in the nominative before too)
== kbx_i.js
@line 1: Feature module i for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js): two-sided features.
@line 2: 
@line 3: Developer reply portal (/developers/reply/)
@line 4:   GET  /api/x/i/status                      what is live: {reply:{ready}, room:{ready, storage}}
@line 5:   GET  /api/x/i/dns?domain=&token=          live DNS TXT check (Cloudflare and Google DNS-over-HTTPS) for
@line 6:                                             "kolaboro-verify=<token>" on the domain or on _kolaboro.<domain>
@line 7:   GET  /api/x/i/verify?id=&domain=&token=   both ownership proofs at once (DNS TXT as above, or <meta name="kolaboro-verify"
@line 8:                                             content="<token>"> on the domain's homepage) plus whether the domain is the
@line 9:                                             project's official domain as published on its named page (batch 9, b9b)
@line 10:   POST /api/x/i/reply                       submit a reply: re-checks DNS or the meta tag, then stores it for human moderation
@line 11:   GET  /api/x/i/replies[?id=XX-000]         approved replies only (moderated in /admin)
@line 12: The token is random (made in the browser), so the public TXT record never names a Kolaboro project: nothing public links a
@line 13: domain to an anonymous project before a moderator approves the reply. Documents never reach us: the browser hashes them
@line 14: (SHA-256) and only the fingerprint, size and type are sent.
@line 15: 
@line 16: Encrypted deal room (/room/)
@line 17:   POST /api/x/i/room                        create {id, wtok_hash}
@line 18:   GET  /api/x/i/room/{id}?after=n           ciphertext envelopes after seq n
@line 19:   POST /api/x/i/room/{id}                   append {wtok, seq, env}   env = "1.<iv b64url>.<ciphertext b64url>"
@line 20:   POST /api/x/i/room/{id}/delete            delete the room {wtok}
@line 21: The server never sees the key (it lives in the URL fragment). It only accepts envelopes that are base64url ciphertext.
@line 22: 
@line 23: Storage: Supabase SQL in scratchpad m10/i.sql. Until it is applied every write answers 503 {error:'not_ready'} and the pages
@line 24: show "opening soon" (replies) or local-only mode (room).
@line 45: Approximate per-IP hourly limits (Cache API is per data centre, so this is a brake, not an exact quota).
@line 77: opt.want: test hook used only by the local test harness
@line 88: Homepage meta tag: <meta name="kolaboro-verify" content="<token>"> on https://<domain>/ (or www.). Redirects are followed only
@line 89: within the same domain (so a parked page or a redirect elsewhere never counts); at most 512 KB of HTML is read.
@line 130: Is `domain` the project's official domain? The page we track for the project (and its documents page) must live on that domain
@line 131: or one of its subdomains, and must not be a property portal. Two-label public suffixes (co.id, com.au ...) never match alone.
@line 185: ---------- Deal room ----------
@line 190: at least the 16-byte GCM tag plus a little payload
@line 195: ['room', id?, 'delete'?]
@line 205: reject non-ciphertext before anything else, even in local-only mode

@line 38: ---------- public data files, read from the deployed assets (memo per isolate, 5 minutes) ----------

@line 227: price index
== kbx_j.js
@line 1: Feature module j for kolaboro.com: reach. Three ways the database meets a buyer where they already are.
@line 2:   GET  /api/x/j/match?p=ab12,cd34        browser extension overlay on property portals. The extension never sends the page address or
@line 3:                                          title: it hashes them locally and sends only 4-hex-character prefixes of the hashes (k-anonymity,
@line 4:                                          like a password range query). We answer with every key in those buckets and the extension
@line 5:                                          compares full hashes on the device. Keys: truncated SHA-256 of a portal listing address or a
@line 6:                                          project name, built from the research agent's private files (kbx_j_map.js holds hashes only).
@line 7:   POST /api/x/j/tg                        Telegram "forward and check": forward a developer's message, link, brochure photo or PDF to the
@line 8:   GET  /api/x/j/tg/status                 Kolaboro bot and get the desk review back, quotes checked word for word against the source, plus a
@line 9:                                           link to the full result, signed with the Kolaboro Ed25519 key. The result travels in the link's
@line 10:                                           #fragment, so we never store what a buyer forwards.
@line 11:   GET  /api/x/j/push/key                  Web Push alerts: "watch this project". VAPID (RFC 8292) and aes128gcm payload encryption
@line 12:   POST /api/x/j/push/watch | unwatch | mine  (RFC 8291, RFC 8188) in WebCrypto. A daily GitHub Actions job (OIDC, no stored secret)
@line 13:   POST /api/x/j/push/test                 calls /push/run: it compares each watched project's public data with the last state and pushes
@line 14:   POST /api/x/j/push/run                  the change. GET /api/x/j/push/selftest runs the RFC 8291 Appendix A test vector live.
@line 15:   GET  /api/x/j/push/status
@line 17: batch 18 (n18a): developer website health in the daily alert state
@line 27: fixed-window counter in the edge cache (per data centre: a soft limit, the processing spend cap is the hard one)
@line 61: =====================================================================================================================
@line 62: 9. Extension overlay: hash-prefix lookup
@line 63: =====================================================================================================================
@line 91: =====================================================================================================================
@line 92: 9b. Kolaboro X-ray extension 2.x (f14a): the same k-anonymity range query, now against the NAMED records (kbx_named.js data),
@line 93:     so the panel can say which project or developer this is and link its named page.
@line 94:   GET /api/x/j/xr?p=ab12,cd34   up to 500 prefixes -> { v: 2, hits: [{h, k, r}], refs: {r: record} }
@line 95:   GET /api/x/j/xr/meta          real key counts by kind
@line 96: Keys (SHA-256 hex, first 16 chars, computed on the device by the extension the same way):
@line 97:   u|<host+path>   exact page: the project page we track, or a portal page about exactly one project (our sources + kbx_j_map)
@line 98:   d|<domain>      registrable domain of a developer's own site (portals, social sites and any domain used by 3+ developers excluded)
@line 99:   n|<name>        project name (2+ words, 8+ characters), normalised like the extension's windows()
@line 100:   v|<name>        developer name (6+ characters)
@line 101: Records: p (project: name, developer, market, stage, promised handover, last checked; score only when reviewed by Kolaboro),
@line 102: v (developer: name, number of projects), g (a website that lists several tracked projects). Sumba never appears (data() drops it).
@line 103: =====================================================================================================================
@line 114: n31e (batch 31, item 157): transparency score in the extension panel
@line 132: exact pages: the tracked page itself, plus portal pages in our sources that are about one project only
@line 137: developer websites by registrable domain
@line 155: the research agent's older portal-address table (hash only) for the same projects
@line 180: =====================================================================================================================
@line 181: 10. Telegram: forward and check
@line 182: =====================================================================================================================
@line 206: the same address rules as the site's desk review: public http(s) hosts only
@line 224: a quote counts as verbatim only if it appears in the source text, ignoring case, spacing and quote styles
@line 228: TRANSCRIBE: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 254: what a buyer can forward: returns { kind, label, source text, html for the reviewer, input fingerprint, url? }
@line 284: must match gen_map.py url_key()
@line 315: only what the buyer's own document says; nothing from our anonymous records
@line 354: Telegram retries a webhook that is slow to answer: each update is handled once
@line 369: =====================================================================================================================
@line 370: 13. Web Push: VAPID (RFC 8292) + aes128gcm message encryption (RFC 8291 / RFC 8188), all in WebCrypto
@line 371: =====================================================================================================================
@line 381: RFC 8291 section 3: encrypt one push message. opts.salt / opts.asPrivate / opts.asPublic are only for the published test vector.
@line 396: record size 4096: one record, the payload is far smaller
@line 404: WebCrypto gives raw r||s, which is what JWS ES256 wants
@line 416: RFC 8291 Appendix A, run live: same keys and salt must give the same bytes, byte for byte
@line 423: and a VAPID signature that verifies with the public key we publish (only when the sending key is installed)
@line 456: a confirmation push straight away, so the buyer knows alerts reach this device (and we know the encryption works end to end)
@line 473: ---------- daily job: what changed in each watched project's public record ----------
@line 485: plain-language list of what changed, most important first
@line 487: n27e (item 79): the developer company appears in an official gazette notice of insolvency or dissolution (states saved before this
@line 488: field existed have no 'lists' and only record it, so the first run after the change sends nothing for old notices)
@line 527: n27e: official notices about the developer company
@line 531: first sight of a project only records its state
== kbx_j_map.js
@line 2: Generated by gen_map.py from the agent's private project files. Truncated SHA-256 of "u|host/path" or "n|name" -> anonymous id.
@line 3: Only hashes: no addresses or names are stored here. Keys mapping to more than one project are dropped.
@line 4: n40 k5: in /data/i18n/kbx_j_map.json
== kbx_k.js
@line 1: Feature module k for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js): API keys and metering, metered open API, Ask Kolaboro (cited answers only).
@line 2: Helpers come from kolaboro.js; nothing here calls a model except through claude() (daily spend cap).
@line 11: n31t: burst limit per free key
@line 19: Per-IP hourly counter in the Cache API (same pattern as kolaboro.js). Returns true when the limit is reached.
@line 43: ---------------------------------------------------------------- API keys
@line 44: The key is generated here, shown once, and only its SHA-256 goes to the database. Tables and functions: scratchpad m10/k.sql.
@line 56: label is optional
@line 57: honeypot field
@line 79: Metered copy of the open API: /api/x/k/m/<path> answers exactly like /api/open/<path> (same body, same signature) and counts one call.
@line 85: n31t (batch 31, item 266): a per-minute burst limit per free key on top of the daily quota (counted in the edge cache before the
@line 86: database is asked, so a runaway client cannot hammer it); a free key gets KEY_PER_MIN calls a minute
@line 103: ---------------------------------------------------------------- Ask Kolaboro
@line 104: Retrieval over kolaboro.com's own pages (ask/index.json, built by ask/build_index.py) and the open dataset (/api/open/*).
@line 105: The model sees only the retrieved passages. Each sentence must name one passage and quote it; the worker then checks the
@line 106: quote is really in that passage, that every number in the sentence is in that passage, and that the sentence's words are
@line 107: mostly the passage's words. Anything that fails is removed. If nothing survives, the answer is "I don't know".
@line 143: pages only if the dataset is unavailable
@line 144: BM25 statistics
@line 172: ASK_SYSTEM: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
== kbx_l.js

@line 56: external <script src> on key pages: which carry Subresource Integrity
@line 58: loaders whose code changes by design: SRI cannot be pinned
@line 71: status from the evidence and its age
@line 87: 1. Backups and restore drill
@line 94: 2. Uptime monitoring
@line 101: 3. Encryption in transit: measured from outside Cloudflare by the deploy guard (a request from inside the edge skips the edge's own HTTPS redirect)

@line 124: 6. Daily evaluation
@line 131: 7. Security scanning
@line 139: 8. Decoy credentials
@line 146: 9. Admin access control

@line 166: 12. Change log and safe deploys
@line 184: ---------- Investor dossier: metrics computed from existing open data only ----------

@line 234: cost per review needs spend for the same 7 days as the review count; until 7 days of spend exist it is not computed
== kbx_land.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com batch 19 (n19c): rules on the land. No model calls, no keys.
@line 2: Data (built on main by .github/workflows/kolaboro-land-rules.yml, tools/kolaboro-agent/land_rules.py; same projects and pins as
@line 3: /data/construction-progress.json):
@line 4:   /data/land-rules.json   protected and restricted land at and near each pin: Natura 2000 (EEA, CC BY 4.0), England planning data
@line 5:                           (green belt, conservation areas, SSSI, National Landscapes, national parks, SAC, SPA, Ramsar, ancient

== kbx_lastmod.js
@line 2: True lastmod for kolaboro.com named project pages (batch 15, s15b).
@line 3: "Last modified" means the date of the last REAL change of the data a page shows, never the date we last checked it or the deploy date.
@line 4: For every project we fingerprint five parts of its data, with volatile dates (checked, seen, sealed, invited) and page-copy hashes
@line 5: (sha, fp: a re-read developer page whose text hash moved but whose extracted facts did not; copies are shown on /changes/) removed:
@line 6:   n = its entry in /data/named.json (names, facts, claims, sources)   c = its case in /data/bali-cases.json (checks, score, stage, history)
@line 7:   p = its dated promises in /data/promises.json                       f = its live findings in /data/findings-by-project.json
@line 8:   s = its monthly satellite series in /data/construction-progress.json
@line 9: /data/lastmod.json pins, per project and part, the fingerprint and the date it was first seen. A part whose fingerprint still matches
@line 10: keeps its pinned date; a part that changed since the last pin takes the "updated" date of the data file that now carries the change
@line 11: (the build that published it). Page lastmod = the latest of its parts and of the date it was added. The pin file is refreshed by
@line 12: tools/kolaboro-seo/lastmod.py (Kolaboro SEO workflow) from GET /api/seo/lastmod, which returns exactly this computation.
@line 13: Pure functions plus one loader; no imports, so the same code runs in the worker and in node (the first pins were seeded by replaying the git history of these files).
@line 28: files: { named, cases, prom, find, prog } (parsed JSON, any may be null); pin: parsed /data/lastmod.json or null
@line 52: n20p: the same date for ONE page, from files the caller has already parsed. A named page only needs its own project (a developer
@line 53: page: that developer's projects), so it no longer waits for 6.7 MB to be parsed again and 1,100 projects to be fingerprinted on
@line 54: a fresh isolate. q = { named, cases, caseBy, prom, find, prog, raw(x) } where raw(x) returns the entry exactly as published
@line 55: (kbx_named.js tidies display names in place). Returns null when a file is missing, and the caller falls back to trueLastmod().
@line 73: worker loader: reads the published files once per isolate and data version
@line 75: n20p: true when a file had to come over the network; only then does the result expire
@line 83: the six files come from this deployment's own assets and cannot change while the isolate lives; fingerprinting 1,100 projects
@line 84: again every 5 minutes cost several hundred ms of CPU on the first named page after each expiry (n20p)
@line 99: GET /api/seo/lastmod: the computed state, in the format of /data/lastmod.json (the pin job publishes it as is)
@line 106: Structured data for a rendered named page (called by kbx_named.js before the page is assembled and cached):
@line 107:  - WebPage/ProfilePage and Dataset dateModified = the true lastmod above (the visible "last checked" date is left as it is);
@line 108:  - an ImageObject for the page's 1200x630 share card (rendered by kbx_og.js from the same facts), linked as primaryImageOfPage.
@line 112: n20p: one page's date without the full pass

== kbx_lookup.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com live official-register lookup (batch 7). Routed from index.js before kolaboro.js api():
@line 2:   POST /api/kb/lookup {url?, q?, country?, refcat?, address?, permit?, company?, lat?, lng?, image_hashes?[]}   public, rate limited

@line 8:   GET  /api/kb/lookup/raw/{sha256}  the exact bytes an official source returned (edge cache, 30 days)

@line 11: timestamped in Bitcoin through the site's existing OpenTimestamps path (/api/kb/stamp). Nothing here is invented: a source with
@line 12: no data says not_found / not_published, a source we cannot query says unavailable or needs_key.

== kbx_n33i.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com (n33i, batch 33, 20 Nov 2026): indexing and crawl budget. No paid AI, no DB, no new Actions minutes.
@line 2:   pre(req, u, env)   -> Response | 'nf' | null. Old query-string URLs that Search Console crawls (items 3, 4, 5, 15):
@line 3:                         /p/?id=<ID>       -> 301 /<lang>/projects/<slug>/; Sumba ids -> 301 the transparency ranking; unknown id -> 'nf' (real 404)
@line 4:                         /m/?market=<m>    -> 301 /m/<slug>/; Sumba -> the transparency ranking; unknown market -> 'nf'
@line 5:                         /<lang>/ask-developer/?id= -> 301 /ask-developer/?id= (the form exists in English only; was a 404)
@line 6:                         /explain/, /timeline/, /ask-developer/ with a well-formed but unknown project id -> 'nf' (was 200 with an empty template)
@line 7:   post(req, u, r, env) -> every /api/ answer gets X-Robots-Tag: noindex (item 1); a GET to an unknown /api/kb/ path answers 410 (item 6);
@line 8:                         HTML links to URLs with a query string get rel="nofollow", and /p/?id= and /m/?market= links become clean paths (item 13).
@line 9:   lmBot(req, path, tag, lm) -> the Last-Modified sent to crawlers and whether their If-Modified-Since earns a 304 (item 17, see kbCond in index.js).
@line 20: market slug -> the slug of its /m/<slug>/ page (same rule as kbx_seo.js slugM); Sumba is left out
@line 53: ---------- after the answer ----------
@line 56: -> null (leave), { nf: true } (add nofollow), { p: id } or { m: slug } (clean path)
@line 79: endpoints our pages POST to answer 405, never-existing paths 410
@line 86: synchronous for every other link; only /p/?id= and /m/?market= links wait for the project index
@line 94: n38 d2: wide tables scroll in their box; the box takes keyboard focus so the scroll works without a mouse (WCAG 2.1.1)
@line 97: ---------- 304 for crawlers (item 17) ----------
@line 98: Cloudflare drops the ETag of HTML answers on this zone (checked 20 Nov 2026: static files keep theirs, pages arrive without one), so
@line 99: crawlers revalidate with If-Modified-Since only. The edge remembers, per URL, the fingerprint of the page (nonce-free SHA-1, the
@line 100: same as the ETag) and when it first served that version. The Last-Modified sent to crawlers is the later of the data date and that
@line 101: moment, so a template change also moves it; a 304 is sent only when the fingerprint is unchanged and the crawler's date is not older.

@line 237: September 2024, last phase September 2027"): the later date is used, and a "kept" status (it described the earlier date or phase)

== kbx_named_diff.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com "what changed" viewer for named project pages (batch 9, agent b9b).
@line 2:   GET /projects/<slug>/changes/[?a=<sha256>&b=<sha256>]   side-by-side dated diff of the developer's page text between two of the
@line 3:                                                         versions our agents archived (default: the latest change), changed
@line 4:                                                         sentences highlighted, word-level marks inside them
@line 5:   GET /projects/<slug>/changes/<sha256>.txt             the sealed copy itself: the exact text whose SHA-256 is in the URL
@line 6: Data: /data/page-history-<k>.json (k = sum of the id's character codes mod 3), built without any model call by
@line 7: tools/kolaboro-agent/page_history.py on main from the agents' archive (data/archive/<sha>.txt.gz). Each version is stored as
@line 8: indexes into a list of unique segments; joining them gives back the exact archived text, so the .txt copy hashes to its name.
@line 9: Display only (never the .txt copy): e-mail addresses and phone numbers are masked.
@line 12: r6u (batch 37): page text in 8 languages (en, es, fr, de, ru, zh, ar, nl), /<lang>/projects/<slug>/changes/; the .txt copies stay one address
@line 13: n40 k5: in /data/i18n/kbx_named_diff.json
@line 23: n20p: a shard read from this deployment's assets (a) does not expire (4.3 MB each)
@line 34: summary for the named page: number of versions, first and last day, how many versions changed the text
@line 42: LCS diff of two arrays of strings, after trimming the common prefix and suffix
@line 59: group into rows: equal rows, and change blocks where removed and added sentences are paired side by side
@line 71: word marks inside a changed pair; long sentences show only the words around each change
@line 87: CSS: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 103: keep 2 unchanged sentences of context around each change; fold the rest
@line 108: the right column of an equal row is the same sentence; mark it so mobile shows it once
@line 144: default: the most recent pair whose words differ
@line 152: r6u (batch 37): in 8 languages, /[l/]projects/<slug>/changes/
@line 160: r6u: a project without an archived copy yet answers 200 (noindex) with the explanation, not 404: every project page links here
== kbx_named_i18n.js
@line 1: Page chrome and sentence templates for the named project and developer pages (kbx_named.js), in the site's 8 languages.
@line 2: Only templates are translated. Facts quoted from sources (claims, evidence, what the developer publishes) stay in the
@line 3: wording of the source and are marked lang="en". Placeholders: {n} project, {dev} developer, {place}, {h} date, etc.
@line 4: Legal rule: no opinion words and no verdicts in any language (checked by the scan in kbx_named.js, see OPINION_RX).
@line 7: n32w (19 Nov 2026): the string tables moved out of the worker (Cloudflare Pages limit of 25 MiB for _worker.js) into
@line 8: /_s/kolaboro.com/data/i18n/kbx_named_i18n.json, filled once per isolate by kbx_strings.js before any kolaboro.com request is handled.
@line 9: Edit the strings there (JSON, same keys); never read them at module top level.
== kbx_named_plus.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com named pages, batch 10 (b10c): two sections for kbx_named.js.
@line 2: 1) Price per m2 against official data (project pages). Shown only when the project publishes a starting price and a smallest size
@line 3:    in the currency of an official open dataset; otherwise an honest "no official comparison available". Official sources used:
@line 4:    - UAE, Dubai: Dubai Land Department open data (gateway.dubailand.gov.ae/open-data/transactions, no key): every registered
@line 5:      off-plan residential sale in a 90-day window, median AED per m2 (TRANS_VALUE / ACTUAL_AREA) per DLD project and per DLD area.
@line 6:    - Spain: Ministerio de Vivienda y Agenda Urbana (MIVAU, formerly Fomento) "valor tasado medio de vivienda libre", EUR per m2,
@line 7:      by municipality over 25,000 inhabitants (dwellings up to five years old where published, else all) and by province.
@line 8:      That is the official appraised value, not a sale price; the page says so.
@line 9:    Snapshot below, refreshed by re-running the generator (scratchpad b10c-gen/dld.py and es.py); dates are shown on the page.
@line 10:    Checked and not usable today: Dubai Pulse (needs a key), NYC rolling sales (open, but no tracked NYC project publishes price and
@line 11:    size), INE Spain (index only, no EUR per m2), Notariado portal (no open API), INE Portugal (open bank-valuation series last updated 2023).
@line 12: 2) Company network (developer pages): GLEIF level-2 relationships (direct parent, ultimate parent, direct children) of the LEI the
@line 13:    page already matched, cached at the edge for a week per LEI, plus the developer's other projects we track, drawn as a small graph.
@line 14: Every sentence is a template in kbx_named_i18n.js (keys ppm_* and net_*), so the sections read in the page language (fin11).
@line 17: n31d: DLD registered sale prices are not asking prices

@line 91: size_min_m2, currency } }, as on the named pages. Returns { own, cur, price, size, official: [...], why } (why = reason for no row).
@line 117: ---------------------------------------------------------------- company network (GLEIF level 2)

@line 179: ---------------------------------------------------------------- corporate family (n19e, batch 19)

== kbx_named_qa.js
@line 2: kolaboro.com batch 24 (agent n24s, 12 Nov 2026): what people search about one project, answered at the top of its named page.
@line 3: Templates only (8 languages); every fact comes from the data the page already holds (named.json, bali-cases.json, promises.json,

@line 7: n40 k5: in /data/i18n/kbx_named_qa.json

@line 10: an overdue handover in the title where it fits, descriptions that open with the fact few other pages give (handover date passed N
@line 11: months ago, date moved N times, no permit published), the dated overdue notice on project pages and developer titles with the year.

@line 41: n27g: "<name> reviews <year>: <overdue handover | handover date, permit and developer>", longest form that fits first
@line 44: n32s: f.dn, completed projects
@line 50: a long name without its bracketed part ("Meriva Collection (Gardens / Signature)")
@line 52: still too long: the name up to its first comma, dash or slash
@line 55: n25t: names of 50+ characters: drop the developer's name inside the project name, then words that describe rather than name
@line 56: ("Private", "Resort and", "under construction"), then cut at a word boundary (never ending on "at", "in", "near"...).
@line 57: The searched word ("reviews", "opiniones", "Erfahrungen"...) always stays.
@line 70: Description under 155 characters, most useful facts first; shorter place, then fewer facts until it fits.
@line 73: n27g: the description opens with the fact few other pages give: a handover date that passed, a date moved, no permit published

@line 1: kolaboro.com named project pages: construction progress from space (batch 14, f14b). No model calls.

@line 5: served from the satellite packs by kbsat.js). Shown with the developer's promised dates (/data/promises.json and the
@line 6: record's facts) on the same timeline. Server-rendered SVG, no client script. Projects without a series get no section.
@line 7: n30a (batch 30): before/after slider over two dated images of the same sprite
@line 8: n17b: Sentinel-1 radar and Overture buildings, same circle
@line 10: n18b: construction stage estimate per month (rules), time machine labels
@line 14: n40 k4
@line 24: n40 k4: strings in 8 languages live in /data/media-i18n.json (sat)
@line 34: a promised date or period as [first month index, last month index]
@line 45: milestones: promise tracker first (with earlier dates of moved promises), then the record's facts for kinds it lacks
@line 57: CSS: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 59: change index per month: (built-up or bare share of the circle minus that of the ring) minus the median of that difference over the first
@line 60: 3 clear months, in points. Season and haze move circle and ring together, so the difference is far steadier than either share alone
@line 61: (on our 125 series the single-month deviation in the first months has a median of 4 points and a 90th percentile of 15).
@line 83: milestones (behind the line); labels alternate between two rows when close
@line 85: label extents per row; a label that fits neither row is left out (the shaded band keeps its tooltip)
@line 98: the line joins clear months across cloudy ones (each dot is a real month; cloudy months show in the strip and the table)
@line 106: n18b: time machine (slider over the real monthly thumbnails of the sprite, drawn on a canvas, image loaded only near the viewport)
@line 107: n40 k4: strings in 8 languages live in /data/media-i18n.json (sat_TM)
@line 110: TM_CSS: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 133: rec: one project from construction-progress.json; meta: the whole file. trk(kind) = translated milestone kind, fd(date) = display date.
@line 134: tl(date): the tile's accessible name with the project name (s15a)
@line 149: n18b
@line 151: strip: every month, newest first in the DOM; row-reverse shows them oldest-to-newest and starts scrolled to the newest
@line 161: n18b: the sprite loads only when the section comes near the viewport (TM_JS adds is-on); without script, noscript shows it
@line 181: n29p (batch 29, item 13): the change verdict of the section in one sentence, for the line under the quick answer
@line 182: ('' when the series is too short to say). Same index and band as the section, so the two never disagree.

== kbx_o.js
@line 1: Feature module o for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js): community trust.
@line 2:   POST /api/x/o/witness        an independent witness hands in its cosignature of our log checkpoint (C2SP tlog-cosignature,
@line 3:                                Ed25519). Verified here: key hash, timestamp, signature, and that the cosigned tree is exactly our
@line 4:                                tree at that size. Stored only once the table in m11/o.sql exists; until then: "opens soon".
@line 5:   GET  /api/x/o/witnesses      registered witnesses and their latest cosignature (state "setup" until m11/o.sql is applied)
@line 6:   GET  /api/x/o/checkpoint     our checkpoint with every witness cosignature that matches it appended (a witnessed signed note)
@line 7:   POST /api/x/o/bench/case     one benchmark case with ONE model forced (Haiku or Sonnet), through the production desk review.
@line 8:                                Callable only by .github/workflows/kolaboro-benchmark.yml (GitHub OIDC). Answer signed (Ed25519).
@line 9:   GET  /api/x/o/slo            service level objectives and remaining error budgets, computed from uptime probes, the API log and
@line 10:                                real-user vitals at request time
@line 13: WKEY is read live as K.WKEY (it can come from a secret)
@line 30: ---------- small helpers ----------
@line 43: per-location counter in the Cache API, one hour
@line 49: RFC 9162 tree hash over the daily log lines ("day head"), exactly as /api/open/checkpoint computes it
@line 55: GitHub OIDC: proof that a request comes from one named workflow of calory89/webs-estaticas (no stored secret)
@line 71: ---------- 1. External witnesses ----------
@line 73: OPENS_SOON: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 100: names under our domain are reserved for our own witness workflow
@line 131: ---------- 2. Benchmark: one case, one model ----------
@line 132: The production desk review always asks Haiku first and Sonnet as a second reader. To score each model alone through the
@line 133: SAME code path, a fetch wrapper refuses (HTTP 529, "overloaded") the other model's call, but only for Anthropic requests whose
@line 134: body carries this run's token. The token is the URL fragment of the fixture URL; the page text is untouched. Every other
@line 135: request in the isolate passes straight through.
@line 136: token -> { allow: model id, answered: [], usage: {in, out}, refused: 0 }
@line 158: the same checks as the daily evaluation in kolaboro.js (evCheck), copied so the scoring is identical
@line 211: ---------- 4. SLOs and error budgets ----------
@line 212: Probe schedule documented with /api/open/uptime: database every 5 minutes (288 a day) and GitHub Actions every hour (24 a day).
@line 221: availability
@line 227: failed probes the whole month may contain

== kbx_p_hz.js
@line 2: Natural hazards at a point, read straight from open sources. No account, no key, no imports: the same file runs in the
@line 3: worker (kbx_p.js, live pins) and in the GitHub Actions batch (tools/kolaboro-hazards, tracked projects).
@line 4: Every function returns { ok, ...figures, source, query, limits } or { ok: false, why }. Nothing is estimated or filled in.
@line 8: haversine, km
@line 29: ---------- minimal GeoTIFF reader: first IFD, tiles or strips, deflate or LZW, predictor 1/2/3, float32 or int16 ----------
@line 47: read a small array tag (geo tags); big offset arrays are read on demand
@line 62: entry i of a LONG array tag
@line 72: TIFF LZW (MSB first, early change), table-based; stops once `need` bytes are out
@line 106: floating point predictor: undo byte differencing, then un-shuffle the byte planes
@line 132: ---------- 1. Elevation: Copernicus DEM GLO-30 (AWS Open Data) ----------
@line 154: ---------- 2. Flood depth: WRI Aqueduct Floods v2 (riverine and coastal, 100-year return period, baseline) ----------
@line 172: ---------- 3. Earthquakes: USGS ComCat (FDSN event service) ----------
@line 189: ---------- 4. Tsunami run-ups: NOAA NCEI Global Historical Tsunami Database ----------
@line 208: ---------- 5. Volcanoes: Smithsonian Global Volcanism Program, Volcanoes of the World ----------
@line 226: ---------- 6. Distance to the coastline: OpenStreetMap natural=coastline via the Overpass API ----------
@line 228: point to segment on a local flat projection (fine at these distances)
@line 250: fetched alongside, used only if OSM fails
@line 261: Fallback when every Overpass server fails: Natural Earth 1:10m land outline (public domain, via world-atlas 2.0.2),
@line 262: cut into 10 degree tiles published at /data/coast/<lon>_<lat>.json (every tile exists, open ocean ones are empty, so a 404 or a missing tile is an error); 36 column files /data/coast/<lon>.json (quantised TopoJSON units, delta-encoded lines).
@line 263: Coarse on purpose: the outline is generalised and quantised to a few hundred metres, so the result is given in bands.
@line 265: NE_LIMITS: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 268: one file per 10 degree longitude column, holding its 18 tiles (5 Nov 2026: fewer files for the Pages 20,000 file limit)

== kbx_plural.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com n40 k14 (26 Nov 2026): one shared plural helper for counts in Arabic and Russian.
@line 2: Many templates build "<n> <word>" with one fixed word ("431 مشاريع", "4 521 проектов"). Arabic and Russian change the word with the
@line 3: number: CLDR categories from Intl.PluralRules pick it (ar: two / few 3-10 / many 11-99 / other 100+; ru: one / few / many).
@line 4: word(lang, n, noun) is the helper for new code; fix(lang, html) applies the same rule to a finished page, so every template agrees.
@line 5: Forms: /_s/kolaboro.com/data/i18n/kbx_plural.json (loaded once per isolate). Only the count words listed there are touched.
@line 35: a year, not a count

== kbx_stage.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com batch 18 (n18b): construction stage estimate per month from open satellite and map data. Plain rules, no model,
@line 2: no training, no AI calls. Inputs are the public files the "from space" section already shows:
@line 3:   /data/construction-progress.json  Sentinel-2 L2A monthly change index of the 150 m circle (kbx_named_sat.js index())
@line 4:   /data/satellite-radar.json        Sentinel-1 RTC VV/VH change index, only for cloudy plots (kbx_space.js)
@line 5:   /data/buildings.json              Overture Maps building footprints touching the circle, with GERS first-seen release
@line 6:   developer's promised dates        /data/promises.json and the project record (kbx_named_sat.js milestones())
@line 7: Shown inside the "from space" section of named project pages (kbx_named_sat.js calls stageHtml) and in the time machine.
@line 8: Routes:
@line 9:   GET /api/x/stage/<ID>                          per-month estimate, confidence and reasons for one named project (JSON)
@line 10:   GET /methods/construction-stage/ (+ /<lang>/)  the rules in 8 languages
@line 11: n32w: string tables loaded as static JSON (worker size limit)
@line 20: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ strings (8 languages)
@line 21: n32w (19 Nov 2026): table moved to /data/i18n/kbx_stage.json, filled by kbx_strings.js before any kolaboro.com request is handled
@line 33: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the rules
@line 34: rec: one project of construction-progress.json; ix: kbx_named_sat.js index(rec) (null when fewer than 6 clear months);
@line 35: rd: satellite-radar.json; bd: buildings.json; ms: milestones() of the project (promised dates, old ones flagged).
@line 36: Returns { enough, months: [{ m, s, c, r: [[code, params], ...] }], latest } where s is a stage key, c 'high'|'medium'|'low'|null.
@line 46: buildings: from the month of first sighting (or the release, when the registry has no date) onwards
@line 54: built-up or bare share of the circle at the start
@line 61: optical: median of the last 3 clear months up to this one, read only when this month itself is clear
@line 71: radar: median of the last 3 months with data up to this one
@line 83: reasons, one per signal
@line 99: stage
@line 119: the developer's dates, as context (never raise confidence)
@line 129: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ HTML (inside the "from space" section)
@line 130: CSS: n40 k17, in /data/i18n/k17s.json
@line 141: est: stages() result; fd(date) display date; returns the HTML block (empty when the project has no series)
@line 162: per-month stage words for the time machine (month -> [stage word, confidence word])
@line 169: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ routes
@line 197: ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ method page
== kbx_strings.js
@line 1: kolaboro.com n32w (19 Nov 2026): string tables kept as static JSON instead of worker code.
@line 2: Cloudflare Pages refuses every build when the _worker.js directory passes 25 MiB (18 Nov 2026), so the large string tables of the
@line 3: kbx_*_i18n.js modules (and the rule notes of kbx_buy_notes.js / kbx_buy_c29.js; NT29 is merged into NT, BS29 into BS, the overrides of kbx_facets_i18n into FS) live in /_s/kolaboro.com/data/i18n/<module>.json.
@line 4: Each module keeps its export as an empty object; ready(env) fetches every file once per isolate (env.ASSETS, in parallel) and copies
@line 5: the tables into those same objects, so code that imported them reads the full strings. index.js awaits ready() at the start of every
@line 6: kolaboro.com request, before any module that uses the strings runs. Rule for new code: never read these tables at module top level;
@line 7: put new string tables in a JSON file here (or in /data/<x>-i18n.json loaded by the module itself), not in the worker.
@line 8: Size guard: /_s/kolaboro.com/open/worker-size-guard.sh (run by open/pre-push.sh, fails above 24.6 MiB), documented on /engineering/.
@line 29: module-level tables of code modules (n32w step 2): each module reads TB.<module>_<name>, filled here
@line 41: n33a (batch 33): /delays/, report byline, hub feed titles
@line 42: n33c (20 Nov 2026): search titles and descriptions from the page data
@line 44: n40 k5 (26 Nov 2026): more tables of code modules moved to /data/i18n/<module>.json (worker size limit)

== kbx_t.js
@line 1: Feature module t for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js): network. Buyers and partners talk to the record, and every message is signed.
@line 2:   GET  /api/x/t/status                   which parts are open (tables applied, signing key present)
@line 3:   GET  /api/x/t/reports?project=ID       approved buyer reports, anonymised (never the document hash, key hash or IP)
@line 4:   POST /api/x/t/reports                  a buyer report signed with a passkey (WebAuthn). Proof of purchase is the SHA-256 of the
@line 5:                                          buyer's contract or receipt, computed in the browser: the file never leaves the device.
@line 6:                                          One report per passkey per project. Nothing is public until a moderator approves it.
@line 7:   POST /api/x/t/hooks                    partners (API key holders, /api-keys/) register a webhook URL for watched projects
@line 8:   GET  /api/x/t/hooks                    the key holder's webhooks and the last 100 deliveries
@line 9:   POST /api/x/t/hooks/disable            switch a webhook off
@line 10:   POST /api/x/t/hooks/ping               send a signed "ping" event to any https URL now (needs a valid key; no table needed)
@line 11:   GET  /api/x/t/hooks/sample             a freshly signed sample event, to test verification code against
@line 12:   POST /api/x/t/hooks/run                daily job (GitHub OIDC, workflow kolaboro-webhooks.yml): diff each watched project's public
@line 13:                                          record with the last state (same logic as the push alerts, kbx_j.js) and POST signed events
@line 14: Webhook signature: the open API scheme. x-kolaboro-signature = base64 Ed25519 over "kolaboro-sig-v1\n" + SHA-256 hex of the exact
@line 15: body, key id in x-kolaboro-key-id, keys at /.well-known/kolaboro-keys.json. The body carries timestamp, webhook_id and event id,
@line 16: so a receiver rejects stale (over 5 minutes), misdirected (other webhook id) and repeated (seen event id) deliveries.
@line 17: Tables and functions: scratchpad m11/t.sql. Until applied, reports and webhook registration answer "opens soon".
@line 28: per daily run (subrequest budget); the rest waits for the next run as retries
@line 73: ===================================================================================================== signed buyer reports
@line 74: The browser builds this exact string, hashes it, and the passkey signs SHA-256("kolaboro-report-v1\n" + that hash).
@line 89: honeypot field
@line 121: ===================================================================================================== partner webhooks
@line 199: one HTTP attempt: 10 s timeout, no redirects followed, 2xx = delivered
@line 213: in-run retries: up to 3 attempts (waits 2 s and 6 s); a delivery still failing is retried by the next daily runs, up to 6 attempts
@line 218: a 4xx answer will not change on retry
@line 253: first sight only records
@line 257: failed deliveries from earlier runs, one attempt each

== kbx_u.js
@line 1: Feature module u for kolaboro.com (see kbx.js): land and climate: climate scenarios, protected land, night lights, stalled sites.
@line 2: Owned by block U (batch 4, 4 Nov 2026). Endpoints live under /api/x/u/.
@line 3:   /api/x/u/climate?lat=&lng=    100-year flood depth at a pin: baseline, 2030 and 2050 under RCP4.5 and RCP8.5 (WRI Aqueduct Floods v2)
@line 4:   /api/x/u/protected?lat=&lng=  protected areas and mangroves at or within 1 km of a pin (OpenStreetMap via Overpass, Global Mangrove Watch 2020)
@line 5: Every figure is read live (or from a published index built from the open source) and returned with source, licence, exact query
@line 6: and limits. A source that does not answer is reported as unavailable, never estimated. Pins are rounded to about 10 m and the
@line 7: answer is cached at the edge; coordinates are not stored or logged anywhere else.
@line 19: per-isolate window plus a fixed-window counter in the edge cache (soft, per data centre)
@line 51: ================= 1. Climate scenarios: WRI Aqueduct Floods v2, 100-year return period =================
@line 52: The Aqueduct rasters are classic TIFFs with one LZW strip per row and the IFD plus the strip offset and byte-count arrays at the
@line 53: end of the file. We read the file head (geo tags) and the last 180 kB (IFD and both arrays) once per isolate, then exactly one
@line 54: strip per pin, and decode it with the same reader the baseline card uses (kbx_p_hz.js pixel()), so baseline and future agree.
@line 115: one strip per row: hand the reader this row's offset and length directly, so it fetches only that strip
@line 153: ================= 2. Protected land and mangroves =================
@line 154: a) OpenStreetMap (ODbL): protected areas, nature reserves, national parks and mangrove wetlands that contain the pin or come within
@line 155:    1 km, read from the OpenFreeMap vector tiles (OpenMapTiles schema, zoom 14, layers park and landcover). We tried the Overpass API
@line 156:    first, as the coastline card does, but on 4 Nov 2026 every public Overpass server failed or timed out from our worker and from our
@line 157:    test machine, while the tiles answer from a CDN in well under a second. The tiles carry the class and the name, not the legal tags.
@line 158: b) Global Mangrove Watch v3.0, 2020 extent (CC BY 4.0): the 25 m rasters, reduced by us to a 0.002 degree grid (about 220 m) of cells
@line 159:    that contain any mangrove pixel, published at /data/mangroves/<lon>_<lat>.bin. Builder: tools/kolaboro-protected/gmw_index.py on main.
@line 160: WDPA (Protected Planet) is NOT used: its terms forbid commercial use without written permission from UNEP-WCMC.
@line 169: minimal Mapbox Vector Tile decoder: only the layers in `want`; features with properties and rings
@line 204: current tile URL template from the TileJSON (the planet build changes weekly), kept for 6 hours
@line 226: empty tile (open sea)
@line 242: even-odd test over every ring (holes and multipolygons included), in tile units
@line 250: edges made by clipping at the tile border are not real boundaries: skip them
